The content of this document conforms to the provisions of GB/T 1.1-2020, Part 1: Guidelines for Standardization Work - Structure and Drafting Rules of Standardized Documents.
This document replaces GB 18030-2005 "Information technology - Chinese character encoding". Compared with GB 18030-2005, except for structural adjustments and editorial changes, the main technical changes are as follows:
The scope of application of this document has been expanded (see Chapter 1).
Ten vertical punctuation marks and eight Chinese character components in the double-byte encoding area have been modified, changing their corresponding GB/K 13000 code positions. In addition, six Chinese character components and nine Chinese characters with repeated encoding have been identified (see Appendix D, Appendix A of the 2005 edition).
In the four-byte encoding area, 18 GB/T 13000 code positions were modified (see Appendix D, Appendix D of the 2005 edition);
The appendix "Additional Chinese characters and radicals/components" has been deleted; see Appendix 0 in the 2005 edition.
Added the implementation level " (see Section 9 draft);
An appendix "General Normative Chinese Characters Table: Code Positions of Characters" has been added; see Appendix A.
Please note that some of the content in this document may involve patents. The issuing authority of this document does not assume responsibility for identifying patents.
This document was proposed and compiled by the Ministry of Industry and Information Technology of the People's Republic of China.
The release history of this document and the document it replaces is as follows:
It was first issued as GB 18030-2000 in 2000, with the first revision in 2005. This is the second revision.
GB 18030-2022
Information Expansion Technique "Chinese Character Set Encoding"
1 This document specifies the Chinese graphic characters for information technology and their hexadecimal representation of binary codes.
This document applies to the processing, exchange, storage, transmission, display, input, and output of information containing Chinese characters and other graphical and symbolic characters.
This document applies to technical products with functions for the information processing and exchange of Chinese characters and other text and graphical characters. This includes, but is not limited to, software products represented by input methods, optical character recognition (OCR), editing and proofreading, machine translation, speech synthesis, text transcription, and intelligent writing, as well as portable products represented by computers, communication terminals, e-readers, and learning devices.
2 Referenced Documents
The content of the following documents is indispensable to this document and is referred to by the normative references in the text.
Among them, referenced documents with dates indicated apply only to the version corresponding to that date in this document; referenced documents without date indications apply the latest edition (including all amendments) of this document.
GB/T 2312-1980 "Information Exchange Chinese Character Encoding Character Set" Basic Set
GB/I 11383-1989 Information Processing - Coding Rules and Structure for Eight-bit Code for Information Exchange
GB/13000 Information Technology - Universal Combined 8-bit Coded Character Set (UCS)
3 Terms and Definitions
The following terms and definitions apply to this document.
3.1 Character
A single element in a collection of elements used for organizing, controlling, or representing data.
3.2 Coded Character
Characters (3.1) and their encoding.
3.3 User-defined private use area
Users who use products compliant with this document can specify certain areas themselves.
3.4 Vocabulary Repertoire
A set of characters specified by a coding space (3.2)
3.5 Reserved Zone
Reserve for the future the area specified in this document.
GB 18030-2022
4 Vocabulary
4.1 Summary
The characters included in this document are encoded in single-byte, double-byte, or quadruple-byte formats.
The single-byte section includes all 128 characters from 0x00 to 0x7F specified in GB/T 11383-1989.
4.3 Two-Byte Section
The two-byte section includes all graphic characters from GB/T 2312-1980, the CJK Unified Ideographs from GB/T 13000, and some additional graphic characters.
The characters in the double-byte part shall comply with the provisions of Appendix A.
Among them, the graphical representation, code position, and function of the ideographic descriptor should comply with the provisions of Appendix B.
Note: GB/T 13000 unifies the encoding of Chinese characters used in countries and regions such as China, Japan, Korea, and Vietnam.
Chinese characters with unique abstract shapes are assigned separate code positions; Chinese characters with the same abstract shape but different origins are assigned shared code positions.
Encoded Chinese characters are called CJK Unified Ideographs, where CJK stands for China, Japan, and Korea.
4.4 Four-Byte Section
The four-byte section includes 66 CJK Unified Ideographs from GB/T 13000 that are not covered by the above two-byte characters (from 9FA6 to 9FEF, excluding the 8 characters from 9FB4 to 9FBB), as well as CJK Unified Ideographs Extension A, CJK Unified Ideographs Extension B, CJK Unified Ideographs Extension C, CJK Unified Ideographs Extension D, CJK Unified Ideographs Extension E, CJK Unified Ideographs Extension F, and characters of minority languages that have already been encoded in GB/T 13000.
The characters in the four-byte part shall be in accordance with the provisions of Appendix C.
5 Overall Structure
Any number preceded by 0x in the main text is in hexadecimal format, while those without 0x are in decimal format.
The hexadecimal system is used for all encoded representations in the appendix, while all other numerical representations are in decimal.
The single-byte part uses the encoding structure specified in GB/T 11383-1989, using code positions from 0x00 to 0x7F.
The double-byte GB part uses two eight-bit binary byte sequences to represent a character. The first byte range is from 0x81 to 0xFE, and the second byte ranges are 0x40 to 0x7E and 0x80 to 0xFE respectively.
The four-byte part uses 0x30-0x39 as a suffix for the extension of the double-byte encoding, which is not adopted in GB/T 11383-1989. The encoding range is 0x81308130 to 0xFE39FE39.
The first byte of a four-byte character is encoded in the range 0x81-0xFE; the second byte is encoded in the range 0x30-0x39; the third byte is encoded in the range 0x81-0xFE; and the fourth byte is encoded in the range 0x30-0x39. That is: 0x81308130-0x81308139; 0x81308230-0x81308239; 0x8130FEFE30-0x8130FE39; 0x81318130-0x81318139; ... 0xFE39FE30-0xFE39FE39.
The overall structure and code range allocation are shown in Figure 1 and Table 1.
The order of arranging 6 characters
6.1 Order of Character Arrangement for Single-Character Brushing
The single-character section should arrange the characters in the order specified by GB/T 11383-1989.
6.2 Order of Characters in the Dual-Character City Section
The order of characters in the lower two bytes should comply with Appendix A.
6.3 Order of Characters in the Four-Character Brush Section
From 0x81308130 to 0x8439FE39, a total of 50,400 code points. These correspond to characters in the Basic Multilingual Plane of GB/T 13000 that are not included in the double-byte section. The characters are arranged in the order specified by the corresponding characters in the Basic Multilingual Plane of GB/T 13000.
The total number of code points from 0x90308130 to 0xE339FE39 is 1,058,400, which corresponds to the 16 auxiliary planes defined in GB/T 13000. The characters are arranged in the exact order specified by the corresponding code points of the 16 auxiliary planes in GB/T 13000.
The order of the four-byte character sequence should comply with Appendix C.

8 Explanation of Characters and Codes
Compared with GB 18030-2005, some character shapes and/or corresponding GB/T 13000 code points in this document have been adjusted (see Appendix D).
9.1 General Rules
This document specifies three implementation levels.
System software products that comply with the corresponding implementation level should provide input and output functions for all characters within the scope of the corresponding implementation level.
9.2 Level 1 Implementation
Implement level 1 support for the single-byte encoding section, the double-byte encoding section, and the four-byte encoding section of CJK Unified Ideographs (i.e., 0x82358F33 to 0x82359636) and CJK Unified Ideographs Extension A (i.e., 0x8139EE39 to 0x82358738).
Any product subject to the applicability of this document should meet the requirements for Level 1.
Note: According to the software application needs, level 1 can also choose any one or a combination of non-Chinese scripts listed in Table 3.
9.3 Implementation Level 2
Level 2 implementation includes Level 1 implementation.
In addition, level 2 includes all the encoded Chinese characters in the "General Standardized Chinese Character Table" that are not included in level 1.
The code positions and character forms of the Chinese characters included in the "General Standardized Chinese Characters Table" are shown in Appendix A.
System software and supporting software should meet the requirements of level 2.
Note: System software and support software include but are not limited to operating systems, database management systems, middleware. (Information on software product classification is referenced from GB/T 36475.)
9.4 Implementation Level 3
Level 3 implementation includes Level 2 implementation.
Furthermore, achieving level 3 includes all Chinese characters specified in this document and the Kangxi radicals listed in Table 3.
The products used for government services and public services should meet the requirements of level 3.
Note: The service industry, including government services and other service sectors, encompasses but is not limited to the railway transportation industry, road transportation industry, waterway transportation industry, air transportation industry, multimodal transport and transport agency industry, postal industry, financial services, insurance industry, land and property management industry, healthcare, state organs, social security, etc. (Industry classification information is referenced to GB/T 4754).

This page is excerpted from the complete documentation of the GNU C compiler, focusing on explaining the meaning of the options.
Unless someone volunteers to maintain them, the EURo documents and Info documents are authoritative documents.
If there is a contradiction between the manual page and the software, please check the Info.
If we find that the content of this manual page is causing obvious confusion and complaints due to being outdated, we will stop publishing it. There is no other choice. It is impossible to update the Info file and the man manual at the same time, because we do not have time to do this other maintenance work for GNU cc. The GNU project considers the man manual an outdated product and believes that time should be spent on other things.
If you need the complete and latest documentation, please refer to the Info file "gee" or the "Using and Porting GNU CC (for version 2.0)" manual (ft&/HAIZE#H GNU CC 2.0). Both are derived from the original Texinfo file gee.texinfo.
DESCRIPTION: The C and C++ compilers are integrated. They each use one or more of the four steps to process the input files: preprocessing, compilation, assembly, and linking.
The suffix of the source file identifies the language of the source file, but for the compiler, the suffix controls the default settings:
gcc treats the preprocessed file (.i) as a C file and sets up C-style linking.
g++ treats the preprocessed file (".i") as a C++ source file and uses C++ linking format.
The suffix of the source file indicates the language type and the subsequent operations:
.c C source program; Preprocessing, compilation, assembly
C/C++ Source Code; Preprocessing, Compilation, Assembly
cc C++ source code; preprocessing, compilation, assembly
CXX C++ Source Program; Preprocessing, Compilation, Assembly
Objective-C source program; preprocessing, compilation, assembly
Preprocessed C files; compile, assemble
Preprocessed C++ file; compile, assemble
Assembly Language Source Program; Assembly
Assembly language source program; preprocessing, assembly
Preprocessor file; usually does not appear as a later name on the command line. Files that are passed to the linker (Linker) usually include:
Object file
Archive file
Unless the -cec, -s, or -E options are used (or a compilation error prevents the complete process), linking is always the final step.
In the linking phase, all .o files corresponding to the source program, -l library files, and unidentified filenames (including the specified .o and .h header files and .a library files) are passed to the linker options in the order specified on the command line.
Options must be given separately: completely different from `-d -r`.
Most of the -=E : and `-W' options have two opposite formats: -fname and -fno-name (or -Wname and -Wno-name).
Here, only the formats that are not the default options are listed.
Here is a summary of all the options, grouped by type. Explanations are provided in the following chapters.
Code Generation Option
Overall Option
Clearly specify the language of the input file as "language" (instead of the default selection derived from the filename suffix).
This option applies to all subsequent input files until the next '-x' option.
Close any explicit specification of the language, so that the file is processed according to the suffix in the filename with the -x option.
If only four stages (preprocessing, compilation, assembly, and linking) are performed, you can use the `-x' option (or the filename followed by a suffix) to tell GCC where to start. You can use the `-c', `-s', or `-E' options to tell GCC where to stop. Note that certain combinations of options (such as `-x cpp-output -E') may cause GCC to compile or assemble the source file. By default, GCC replaces the source filename suffix with `.o' when generating object files, and the `.s' suffix for assembly files. You can use the `-o' option to specify other names. GCC ignores any input files that follow the `-S' option that it cannot recognize.
the target file has been generated
Stop after compilation; no assembly is performed.
Using pipes instead of temporary files between different stages of the compilation process, because those systems' assemblers cannot read data from pipes. The GNU assembler does not have this problem. The assembler can accept a C "dialect": support for ANSI-standard C programs.
This will disable certain non-ANSI C compatible features in GNU C, as well as features such as unix and vax that are now rarely used and are part of the ANSI trigraph feature. It also disables the use of the standard library even if it is available in the hosted environment, where the main function must return an int value, which is typical for almost all programs except the kernel. In a free-standing environment, the standard library is not available, and the entry point is not necessarily main. A clear example is the operating system kernel. For more details, refer to the latest information on the GCC website.
For function declarations without parameters, such as `int foo ();', treat them in the C style --- that is, do not specify the number or type of parameters. (This applies only to C++.)
In normal circumstances, such a function foo in C++ means that the parameters are empty.
The list of features is cut here, trying to support some aspects of traditional C compilers.
See the NU C manual for details. We have removed the specifics so that people will not be confused when the content becomes outdated.
Except for one thing: for C++ programs (not C), the '-traditional' option has an additional effect, allowing 'this' to be assigned.
It has the same effect as the `-Ethis-is-variable' option.
-traditional-cpp attempts to support some aspects of the traditional C preprocessor.
Particularly the content mentioned above regarding the preprocessor, but does not include the effects of other options except for '-traditional'.
-fdollars-in-identifiers allows the use of the '$' character in identifiers (only for C++).
You can specify the "-Efno-dollars-in-identifiers" option to explicitly prohibit the use of '$' as an identifier.
The GNU C++ compiler by default allows the $' character on some target systems, but not on all systems.
-fenum-int-equiv allows implicit conversion from ant type to enumeration type (enumeration) in MEF C++.
Under normal circumstances, GNU C++ allows conversion from enum to int, but not the other way around.
The -fexternal-templates option generates smaller code for template declarations (limited to C++). It achieves this by generating a single copy of each template function at the point where they are defined.
In order to successfully use this option, you must mark `#pragma implementation` (definition) or `#pragma interface` (declaration) in all files that use the template.
When compiling with `-fexternal-templates`, all template instantiations are of external type. You must have the required instantiations appear in the implementation file.
This can be achieved through typedef. He references each instance as needed.
Correspondingly, if the default option `-fno-extzernal-templates' is used during compilation, all template instantiations are explicitly set to built-in.
All member functions (except the constructor and the mnew or delete member operators) are considered virtual functions of the class they belong to.
This does not indicate that each call to a member function will go through the internal virtual function table each time.
In some cases, the compiler can determine that a virtual function can be called directly; in such cases, it is called directly.
-fcond-mismatch allows the types of the second and third parameters of a conditional expression to be mismatched.
This expression evaluates to void. -fthis—is-variable allows assignment to this (only in C++).
After merging the user-defined free storage management mechanism into C++, it becomes inappropriate to have an assignable 'this'.
Therefore, by default, assigning to 'this' within a class member function is an invalid operation.
However, for backward compatibility, you can enable this operation using the `-fthis-is-variable' option.
-funsigned-char defines char as an unsigned type, similar to unsigned char.
Each system has its own default char type, which may be either an unsigned char or a signed char.
Ideally, when using data types with signedness, a portable program should always use signed char or unsigned char.
However, many programs have been written to use only simple char types, and it is expected that this is a signed (or unsigned, depending on the target machine on which the program was written) type.
This option and its opposite option make the program work with the corresponding default values.
The type 'char' should always be explicitly defined as either 'signed char' or 'unsigned char', even if it behaves exactly like one of them.
If the "signed" or "unsigned" modifier is not explicitly declared, these options are used to define a signed bitfield or an unsigned bitfield.
By default, bit fields are signed because they inherit from basic integer types such as int, which are signed. However, if the -traditional option is specified, bit fields are always unsigned.
The -fwritable-strings option stores string constants in the writable data segment and does not treat them specially.
This is to be compatible with some old programs. It's a very bad idea to treat string literals as being modified by the 'traditional' option.
Executive Summary
Impact of COVID-19 on the Drug Market
Its impact may be similar to that of the 2008 financial crisis.
The global COVID-19 pandemic has placed the world in an unprecedented crisis.
In many countries, this disease has overwhelmed healthcare institutions, nearly causing them to collapse. The restrictions implemented by governments have caused serious disruptions in the global economy.
For the drug market, the impact of this pandemic is still unclear and difficult to predict, but it may have far-reaching effects.
Some producers may be forced to seek new methods to manufacture drugs because restricting the flow also limits access to precursors and basic chemicals.
Transporters may have to find new routes and methods because they are unable to cross the border due to travel restrictions.
The patterns of drug use and distribution may change, and the government's capacity to respond may also be hindered.
The transformation brought by the novel coronavirus is unprecedented, but we can still draw some lessons from past crises.
After the 2008 economic crisis, some drug users began seeking cheaper synthetic substances, and the pattern of drug use shifted toward injecting drugs.
At the same time, governments around the world have cut budgets related to drugs.
If governments in all countries respond to the current economic recession in the same way, preventive measures and interventions against drug use and related risky behaviors, as well as drug treatment services, may be severely hit.
Furthermore, the priority of intercepting operations and international cooperation may decrease, making traffickers more capable of acting.
Some countries have a large amount of drugs being smuggled through commercial flights, and it is expected that the drug trafficking activities in these countries will be directly affected the most.
Messengers have been placing synthetic drugs such as methamphetamine in their personal backpacks or hidden in their private luggage to carry them. Sometimes the quantities are very large. Due to almost everywhere having implemented restrictions on air travel, the activity of supplying drugs through commercial air travel may be completely interrupted.
Although the international postal supply chain has also been disrupted, the activities of trafficking through the dark web and cargo mail may increase.
In the long run, economic downturns and related containment measures may disrupt the drug market.
Due to the rising unemployment rate and lack of opportunities, the poor and vulnerable groups are more likely to use drugs in harmful ways, develop drug addiction, and turn to illegal activities related to drugs—whether production or transportation.
The drug trafficking organization may take advantage of this situation to provide services to vulnerable groups and recruit new members to expand its ranks.
As the government's capacity to respond decreases, these transformations may quickly take root and become a new reality for many communities.
Expansion and Complexity
Market growth
The population growth pattern partially explains the reasons for market expansion.
The phenomenon of drug use around the world has been increasing, both in terms of total numbers and in terms of the proportion of drug users among the world's population.
It is estimated that there were 210 million drug users in 2009, accounting for 4.8% of the global population aged 15-64, and 269 million drug users in 2018, accounting for 5.3% of this population.
In the past two decades, the rate of increase in drug use in developing countries has been much faster than in developed countries.
This to some extent reflects the differences in overall population growth during this period, with developed countries growing by 7% and developing countries by 28%. It also reflects the rapid growth of young populations in developing countries.
Teenagers and young adults account for the largest proportion among drug users.
In the period from 2000 to 2018, the population of this age group in developing countries increased by 16%, while that in developed countries decreased by 10%.
Urbanization is a driving force of the current and future drug market.
Whether it is developed countries or developing countries, the rate of drug use in urban areas is higher than in rural areas.
Part of the reason for the overall increase in drug use is the large-scale movement of people from rural areas to urban areas. Currently, more than half of the world's population lives in urban areas, compared to 34% in 1960.
Urbanization may be a key factor in the future dynamics of the drug market, especially in developing countries where the trend of urbanization is more pronounced.
The increase in wealth is associated with a rise in drug use, but the people who bear the greatest burden of disease are the poorest around the world. In developed countries, drug use is more common than in developing countries.
Cocaine and other drugs are more closely associated with the more affluent regions of the world.
Similarly, within each country, the prevalence of drug use is higher among the wealthy social stratum.
However, individuals with lower socioeconomic status are more likely to develop addiction disorders.
Data from a few countries suggest a link between harmful drug use patterns and low income and the disease.
These patterns seem less common in more affluent social classes.
The economic constraints caused by the global COVID-19 crisis are likely to exacerbate the risks faced by the most vulnerable groups, including drug users.
For example, changes in the labor market, such as rising unemployment rates, have been associated with an increase in drug use in the past. However, this pandemic has already forced hundreds of millions of people around the world to lose their jobs.
The COVID-19 pandemic may lead to further expansion of the drug market. The pandemic's spread could result in more farmers increasing or engaging in the cultivation of illicit crops, either because the control capacity of national authorities is weakened or because, under economic crisis conditions, more people may be forced to take risks and engage in illegal activities.
The pandemic-related restrictions have led to a reduction in air and land transportation, potentially increasing maritime smuggling. The risk of intercepting such smuggling at sea is low, and the volume of maritime smuggling may be higher than that of air or land smuggling.
Recently, there have been reports of cocaine shipments being directly transported from South America via sea routes to Europe.
Complexity enhancement
The situation of the appearance of non-international controlled substances is stable, but new opioid-like substances with potential hazards are increasing, making the drug market increasingly complex.
In addition to plant-based substances such as marijuana, cocaine, and heroin, hundreds of synthetic drugs have been added, many of which are not subject to international control. The non-medical use of drugs is also increasing rapidly.
The domestic markets of member states introduce approximately 500 new active substances each year.
Currently, most of them are anabolic agents, followed by synthetic cannabis receptor agonists, and a small amount of opioids.
Although the total number of new psychoactive substances has stabilized, the proportions have changed.
In 2014, the total number of new psychoactive substances identified included only 2% that were classified as opioid-like. However, by 2018, this figure had risen to 9%.
Many of the new psychoactive substances similar to opioids are derivatives of fentanyl. Their effects and dangers have been proven, and they have caused excessive death cases in North America. Similar incidents have also occurred in other regions, but to a lesser extent.
In North America, fentanyl is used as a adulterant in heroin and other drugs (including cocaine and methamphetamine), and is also used to manufacture counterfeit opioid medications.
Some evidence suggests that the phenomenon of injecting new psychoactive substances as stimulants is also present in Europe: a study of discarded syringes in six cities in Europe found that many syringes were contaminated with new psychoactive substances that have stimulant effects.
The use of new psychoactive substances may gradually become entrenched among vulnerable groups.
A single type of new psychoactive substance rarely forms a large market.
However, evidence from Europe suggests that synthetic cannabinoids are a serious issue among socially marginalized groups such as the homeless and prison inmates.
Twenty-two countries have reported the use of new psychoactive substances in prisons, with most of these countries identifying synthetic cannabinoids as the main challenge.
Regulation of precursor chemicals often forces drug manufacturers to innovate. Many chemicals commonly used as precursors for the synthesis of drugs such as amphetamine, methamphetamine, and "ecstasy" have been placed under international control.
Drug traffickers and drug manufacturers have always been seeking alternatives, not only substances with looser regulations, but also specially designed chemicals to avoid regulation, known as "custom-made precursors."
Restrictive measures aimed at preventing the spread of COVID-19 are likely to further squeeze drug manufacturers who rely on precursor chemicals.
Evidence from Mexico shows that this has become a reality: reports indicate that in March 2020, a shortage of precursor chemicals for methamphetamine imported from East Asia led to a rise in methamphetamine prices in Mexico and the United States.
The market changes rapidly.
Synthetic drugs have replaced opioids in Central Asia and the Russian Federation
The opiate market in Central Asia and the Russian Federation seemed to change between 2008 and 2018.
The number of opioids intercepted by Russian authorities has decreased by about 80%, and the number of people receiving treatment for opioid use has dropped significantly.
However, the doping market seems to be growing increasingly prosperous.
There is evidence that methamphetamine and various cathinones, including methaqualone and a-pyrrolidinylbenzylpiperidine, are now readily available on the Russian drug market.
According to Russian authorities, the number of secret manufacturing sites for various illegal drugs has increased sharply in recent years, more than doubling in two years, reaching 152 sites by 2018.
Afghanistan and Iraq Methamphetamine Markets Expand
As early as 2012, surveys of personnel in hospitals, prisons, and other institutions had already revealed the significance of methamphetamine in Iraq.
Crystalline methamphetamine has become another major drug of concern, joining fentanyl and tramadol.
A 2015 study further confirmed these findings, with drug users reporting that marijuana is harder to obtain than "fenfluramine" or methamphetamine.
Recently, Iraqi authorities have discovered several methamphetamine processing sites. The Drug Enforcement Administration has expressed concern over the large-scale importation of pseudoephedrine preparations. These processing sites use the preparations as a precursor.
In Afghanistan, the production of methamphetamine seems to have begun in 2014.
Since then, the amount of methamphetamine seized has been steadily increasing.
But the amount seized in the first half of 2019 was 657 kilograms — a sharp increase compared to the previous year.
Large amounts of methamphetamine suspected to originate from Afghanistan have been seized in other countries, which also indicates that the country's production is rising rapidly.
Policy changes and trends
Most jurisdictions that have legalized the medical use of cannabis have seen an increase in cannabis use.
Eleven jurisdictions in Canada, Uruguay, and the United States allow the production of cannabis products for non-medical use.
In most jurisdictions, the use of cannabis has increased since legalization, although the same trend has been observed in other jurisdictions where the non-medical use of cannabis remains illegal.
Among the two states, Colorado and Washington, that were the first in the United States to pass legislation allowing adult use of marijuana, the number of adults using marijuana for non-medical purposes has increased, particularly among those who used it in the past month and those who used it daily or almost daily. This rate has exceeded the national average since 2012.
All indicators show that since 2011, the use of marijuana in Uruguay has increased, and the usage last month almost doubled.
This indicates that the main reason for the increase is the frequent and regular use of marijuana.
Short-term data from Canada also show that at the same time as the legal market expanded from 2018 to 2019, usage rates also increased.
However, in 2019, 40% of marijuana users obtained some products from illegal sources.
Is the sharp decline in the amount of marijuana seized globally due to the legalization of marijuana?
In 2018, the amount of marijuana seized globally reached the lowest level in two decades, primarily due to a decline in seizures in North America, which had dropped by 84% over the past 10 years.
By contrast, the number of seized cannabis seized in other parts of the world during the same period almost doubled. The patterns of seizures indicate that policies aimed at liberalizing the cannabis market have played a key role in the decline.
The regulatory effect of managing new psychoactive substances seems to have some restraining effect, but it is still difficult to evaluate the effectiveness of domestic regulation.
Some countries that adopt different regulatory regimes for new psychoactive substances have shown different prevalence rates of their use.
In some other countries, the use of new psychoactive substances has decreased after domestic legislation was enacted.
Weaker groups are harmed by both legal and illegal drug markets. Medications used for pain management and palliative care, mainly opioids, are primarily available in high-income countries, with uneven distribution across regions.
In 2018, more than 90% of all medically prescribed opioids available for consumption were in high-income countries.
About 50% are in North America, 40% in Europe, and the remaining 2% in Oceania.
The population of these countries accounts for about 12% of the world's total population.
It is estimated that the population of low- and middle-income countries accounts for 88% of the world's population, but they consume less than 10% of the medicinal opioids.
Barriers to accessing medicinal opioids are related to many factors, including legislation, culture, health systems, and prescribing practices.
People who are poorer are more likely to suffer from drug addiction. About 35.6 million people had drug addiction in 2018.
Poverty, limited education, and social marginalization may increase the risk of drug addiction and exacerbate its consequences. Research in high-income countries has confirmed this. In these studies, individuals' adverse socioeconomic trajectories—not just their status at a particular point in time—have been found to be associated with the risk of drug addiction.
Drug use disorders can further exacerbate social and economic disadvantages, increasing the risk of unemployment, poverty, and homelessness and thus reinforcing the cycle of disadvantage. These outcomes may also be linked to discriminatory attitudes and consequences within the criminal justice system.
Women, ethnic minorities, immigrants, people from diverse sexual orientations, persons displaced by armed conflict or disaster, and people living in rural areas may also be disproportionately affected by drug use disorders or may face difficulties in accessing treatment services.
According to the disability-adjusted life year (DALY) indicator, which measures the number of years of life lost due to disability and premature death, the social impact of drug use has become more severe over the past 10 years.
From 2007 to 2018, the global disability-adjusted life years attributable to drug use increased by 17%.
The disability-adjusted life years (DALYs) attributable to hepatocellular carcinoma caused by untreated hepatitis C in intravenous drug users have increased by particularly high amounts—reaching 40%.
The relationship between drugs and violence is complex and it is difficult to determine all the causal relationships between the use of psychoactive substances and violence.
Global limited data suggest that intoxication may be an important factor in murder.
However, in violent incidents, the role of alcohol seems to be greater than that of drugs.
Although drug use may lead to crime, this association can be partially attributed to multiple mediating factors, such as socioeconomic disadvantage and other forms of adversity caused by individual risk, family environment, and peer influence.
The connection between drug trafficking and violence is multifaceted.
If there is already a stable criminal organization, large-scale organized crime activities, including international drug trafficking, may be carried out without violence.
In the short term, the presence and intensity of violence are more determined by certain changes that affect the power balance among organized crime groups rather than the volume of trafficking. For example, changes in the size of the illegal market, the death or imprisonment of well-known criminals, and law enforcement measures that weaken one group compared to another are all factors.
Latest trends
Cannabis is the most commonly used substance, and opioids pose the greatest risk.
It is estimated that 19.2 million people used marijuana in 2018, making it the most widely used drug globally.
By comparison, 58 million people used opioids in 2018.
However, the harms associated with opioids have been overlooked because the number of users is relatively small.
In the estimated 167,000 drug-related deaths in 2017, 66% were caused by these substances. Of the 21 million years of life lost due to disability or premature death attributed to drug use, 50% were caused by these substances.
The non-medical use of synthetic opioids has exacerbated the public health crisis.
In West Africa, Central Africa, and North Africa, the opioid crisis is being exacerbated by tramadol, while in North America it is fentanyl. These two regions have little in common in terms of economy, demographic data, or general drug use patterns, yet both are struggling to combat the opioid crisis fueled by substances that are easily obtainable and cheap to produce.
The use of synthetic opioids such as fentanyl in North America has led to an increase in the number of opioid overdose deaths over the past two decades.
In 2018, of the 67,367 overdose deaths registered in the United States, two-thirds involved fentanyl.
Canada also reported similar results, but the numbers were lower.
Part of the reason why fentanyl has led to overdose deaths is that its effects are unpredictable in the illegal drug market.
There has been significant growth in the market for tramadol not intended for medical use in West Africa, Central Africa, and North Africa.
In this area, an increasing number of patients with opioid use disorder are receiving treatment with tramadol.
In 2017, the amount of tramadol intercepted globally significantly increased, reaching a peak of over 125 tons.
However, in 2018, after India passed new regulations on tramadol, the global seizure amount dropped to 32 tons.
The cause of the crisis in these two places is the simplicity of production and low manufacturing costs, as well as the lack of international regulation of tramadol and many fentanyl analogs or their precursors.
The fact that these substances are available on both the pharmaceutical market and the illegal market exacerbates the crisis in these two regions, making it more difficult to detect and prevent their abuse.
Increasing use of performance-enhancing drugs
The illicit drug field is mainly dominated by cocaine and methamphetamine, and the usage of both substances is on the rise in their main markets.
About 19 million people used cocaine in 2018, as cocaine is quite popular in North America and Western Europe.
About 27 million people used amphetamines that year, of which methamphetamine is the most commonly used amphetamine-type stimulant in Southeast Asia and North America.
According to the majority of available indicators, the use of methamphetamine in these two regions has been expanding for the past two decades.
In some markets, cocaine and methamphetamine can coexist, sometimes acting as substitutes for each other. When the use of one drug increases, the use of the other may decrease. At other times, both drugs may increase or decrease simultaneously to meet the demand of the same market.
COVID-19 increases the risk for injecting drug users.
It is estimated that about 11.3 million people injected drugs in 2018, and about 10% of global HIV infections are caused by injection drug use.
More than 1 million people who inject drugs have been infected with HIV, and more than 5.5 million have hepatitis C.
In 2017, approximately 585,000 people died from drug-related causes, with half of them dying from liver disease caused by hepatitis C. Most hepatitis C infections among injection drug users have not been treated.
5 The potential shortage of opioids caused by coronavirus restrictions may lead users to substitute them with more readily available substances such as alcohol or benzodiazepines, or to mix opioids with synthetic drugs.
With some users switching to injection or injecting more frequently, there may be more harmful patterns of use.
Redmi AirDots 2 True Wireless Bluetooth Earbuds
User Manual
Product Introduction
Please read this manual carefully before using the product and keep it in good condition.
The earphones come with medium-sized ear pads. If you find them uncomfortable, you can adjust them to a smaller size.
01 Wear earphones
Push the earpad gently downward into the ear canal at an angle, and stop when the headphones can be lightly shaken without moving.
When talking on the phone, holding the headset microphone toward your mouth can make the call clearer.
When switching between calls, please adjust the headset so that the microphone is exposed.
Before charging and turning on the device, please remove the adhesive strips on the left and right earphones at the charging points. Charge the charging case and the earphones fully.
Plugging in the charging cable can charge both the earphones and the charging case at the same time.
The charging box's charging indicator light remains red and stays on when it is not fully charged. Once fully charged, the charging indicator light turns off.
The earphones are charging. When the earphone indicator light stays red and on, it means the earphones are fully charged. After one minute, the indicator light on the device turns white and stays on for a minute before turning off.
02 Charge and Power On
The earphones are inside the charging case. When you take the earphones out of the charging case, they will automatically turn on.
The earphones are not in the charging case and are off. Press and hold the multifunction button for about 1 second (the white light stays on for 1 second).
Hold for about 1 second
White light stays on for 1 second
Power off
The earphones automatically shut down when placed in the charging case.
Hold the multifunction key for about 5 seconds while the device is powered on (the red light stays on for 2 seconds)
Long press for about 5 seconds

03 Start using
Auto Connect: Two earphones are connected from the charging case when it is taken out.
Two monsters automatically level up, and the white light turns on.
Then the red and white lights resumed. The left and right lamps were connected in series.
Wait for the left/right earbud white light to flash. Then, turn on the device and search for "Redmi AirDots 2" and click to connect.
After the first connection, when you turn on the device, it can automatically reconnect to the previously paired device (Bluetooth must be enabled).
Note: Do not mix this product with any other products.
If the connection fails, put the earphones back into the charging case. Repeat the above steps.
Hold for about 1 second to turn on
Earphone Indicator Light
Single ear / dual ear switching; once both earphones are successfully connected, they are in dual ear mode. If you put either earphone back into the charging case to turn it off, the other earphone will switch to single ear mode. Once you take it out again, it will automatically switch back to dual ear mode.
Manual connection: When the earphones are turned off, manually turn on both earphones. The white light will illuminate, followed by the red and white lights flashing rapidly. Then, the left and right earphones will automatically connect. Wait until the left/right earphones have the white light flashing slowly, and then turn on the device's Bluetooth to search and connect.
Earphone Reset
If the headphones cannot be used properly, please refer to the following method to reset them.
Earphones: Remove the earphones from the charging case. When the device is powered off, press and hold both earphones' function buttons for 15 seconds. After the red and white lights blink alternately three times, release them and place them back into the charging case (to clear the pairing records between the earphones and the phone).

EU Summit Sparks Debate Over Refugee Policy; EU Officials Say Reception Process Faces Numerous Bottlenecks - China News Society
The EU summit is embroiled in fierce debates over refugee policy. EU officials say that every step of the reception process is plagued by bottlenecks.
【Xinhua Correspondent Niu Ruifei of the Global Times in Belgium】According to AFP's report on February 10, at the latest EU summit that day, EU Commission President Von der Leyen said that EU leaders agreed to adopt stricter policies on illegal immigration, making it easier to deport illegal immigrants.
According to reports, this move by the EU is a response to the growing issue of illegal immigration in Europe. Now, the core problem for the EU is that very few of the illegal immigrants can be repatriated.
According to the summit document, EU leaders called on the Commission to "immediately mobilize a large amount of EU funds" to strengthen the external borders through means such as infrastructure and surveillance.
However, the EU and its member states are in constant dispute over the payment of fence-building costs.
Reinhardt repeatedly stated that the EU funds will not cover the related costs.
However, some member states' foreign officials said that if the EU could take on part of the cost of installing cameras, watchtowers, and other infrastructure along the external borders, it would free up national budgets for member states, allowing the construction process to accelerate.
According to the Euractiv website, EU officials admit that there are bottlenecks at every step of the refugee reception process. Limited reception capacity and overwhelmed immigration authorities in member states are unable to prevent refugees from submitting "numerous" asylum applications in various EU countries. The EU's refugee reception system is nearly in a "permanent crisis."
The European Commission estimates that more than 920,000 asylum applications were received by Schengen countries in the EU last year, a nearly 50% increase compared to the previous year.
At the same time, the EU recorded more than 330,000 cases of illegal entry last year.
In this context, the summit also reached an agreement on a "principle" that EU member states may return illegal immigrants to their home countries based on a court decision from another member state.
Additionally, it is reported that EU leaders also agreed to "increase the use of the concept of safe countries," which would pave the way for the EU to establish a common list.
(Source: Global Times, Author: Niu Ruifei)
U.S. Escalates "Chip War" Severely Impacts Global Semiconductor Industry - China News Society
Recently, the "chip war" between the United States and China has been escalating. First, under American pressure, the Netherlands and Japan have agreed to start restricting the export of semiconductor manufacturing equipment to China. Then, foreign media reported that the U.S. government is considering cutting off all ties between American suppliers and Huawei, prohibiting American suppliers from providing any products to Huawei. The Biden administration is also preparing to issue an executive order to restrict U.S. investments in sensitive Chinese tech industries. Soon after the start of 2023, the United States has already launched a series of harsh measures to curb the development of China's semiconductor-related industries.
In recent years, the United States has not ceased its efforts to hinder China's semiconductor development. On one hand, it has precisely targeted China's semiconductor supply chain; on the other hand, it has built a "de-Chinaization" semiconductor supply chain alliance, aiming to completely distance China from the advanced semiconductor industry.
Whether it is luring South Korea to establish a "technological alliance," or forming a "chip quadrilateral alliance" with South Korea, Japan, and Taiwan, or building a "semiconductor supply chain 'small circle'" through mechanisms such as the "Quad" and the "Indo-Pacific Economic Framework," the United States is introducing politicization, factionalization, and militarization into the semiconductor industry. It aims to build a closed supply chain cycle dominated by the United States, thereby compensating for its shortcomings in chip manufacturing and maintaining its dominant position in the global semiconductor industry.
However, the U.S. bold "chip strategy" against China is also a case of "killing one and losing eight."
Recently, Applied Materials, a well-known chip manufacturing equipment supplier in the United States, announced that due to the latest round of export restrictions on chip manufacturing equipment to China, the company will lay off 1,300 full-time employees and plans to lay off another 1,400 temporary employees in the next two quarters.
Recently, ASML Holding, the global leader in photolithography machines, has warned that banning the export of semiconductor manufacturing equipment to China could lead to a rise in semiconductor prices.
Media analysis: After the U.S. government has imposed harsh pressure on China's chip and semiconductor industries, many American companies have been severely affected, including electronic design automation (EDA) tool developers, chip designers, wafer manufacturing equipment producers, and chip manufacturers themselves.
The so-called suppression of China's advanced semiconductor industry by the United States has actually dragged the global semiconductor industrial chain and supply chain down with it.
The semiconductor industry is a high-tech and knowledge-intensive sector that has already formed a closely connected global semiconductor industrial chain.
According to estimates by professionals, based on specialized division of labor, the entire production process of semiconductor products requires crossing national borders more than 70 times, with the entire process taking as long as 100 days.
Although the United States has a strong foundation in the semiconductor industry, it is relatively weak in manufacturing capabilities, especially in advanced process technologies, and heavily relies on the East Asian region.
Even if the United States strongly guides the advanced chip supply chain to shift towards a regionalized development dominated by the United States, it is difficult to change the regional distribution and mutual dependence situation of the global semiconductor industry.
The United States is strongly "reshaping" and "de-Chinese-izing" the semiconductor supply chain, severely impacting the global semiconductor industry.
Chips are the core of the global future industry. The development of the chip industry cannot be separated from the joint participation of countries around the world.
In recent years, the United States has taken a series of unilateral actions, successively passing the "Innovation and Competition Act," the "2022 U.S. Competition Act," and the "Chip and Science Act." It has used its technological hegemony to consolidate its own technological leadership. By employing a mix of benevolence and coercion toward its allies, it has lured chip manufacturers such as Intel, TSMC, and Samsung to establish factories in the U.S. The goal is to firmly ensure the United States maintains global leadership in the chip industry.
In the short term, the United States has indeed caused trouble for China's chip industry and related industries, and has gained real benefits.
But in the long run, America's hysterical suppression will force China's chip industry to continuously break through. This will make China lose a huge market, ultimately greatly weaken the innovation willingness and ability of American tech companies, and harm the development of the global chip industry.
Gao Qiao
First Batch of Individual Pension Product Funds Launches Sales, Minimum Holding Period as Short as 360 Days - China News Society
The first batch of individual pension financial products are now on sale.
The institution's 7 products can be selected, with the shortest holding period being 360 days.
Personal pension investment products have finally been launched.
On February 10th, the China Wealth Management Network released the first list of individual pension product listings. Seven individual pension products from the Industrial and Commercial Bank of China (ICBC) Wealth Management, Agricultural Bank of China (ABC) Wealth Management, and China Post Savings Bank Wealth Management officially started sales.
By now, the "shelf" of individual pension accounts has finally gathered the four types of products: individual pension savings, individual pension investment, individual pension insurance, and public offering funds for individual pensions.
According to the introduction, the first batch of individual pension fund products are all public offering net value-type open-ended products. Among them, 5 new products and 2 existing products are included. Except for one product which is a hybrid type, the remaining 6 products are all fixed income products.
Beijing Youth Daily reporters saw that among the five new products issued, four came from the Industrial and Commercial Bank of China's wealth management department, all of which are fixed income products. The shortest holding periods were 365 days, 540 days, 720 days, and 1080 days, with corresponding performance benchmarks (annualized) of 3.70% - 4.20%, 4.10% - 4.60%, 4.40% - 4.90%, and 5.25% - 5.75%, respectively.
The remaining new product is "Youting Wealth Addition · Hongjin Short Holding 1095 Days No.1", which is also a fixed income product. It started fundraising on February 10th and ended on February 19th. Its risk rating is Level 2.
Among the two continuing products, one is the "Nongyin Tongxin · Lingdeng" 360-Day Innovation Technology RMB Wealth Management Product by Nongyin Securities, which is a hybrid type of investment and has a risk level of Level 3.
Another surviving product is "Youyin Wealth Tianti · Hongjin Shortest Holding 365 Days No.1", a fixed income type product. It started fundraising on December 30 last year and officially commenced operations on January 4 this year. Its cumulative net value was 1.0011 on February 8.
According to the Opinions of the Office of the State Council on Promoting the Development of Personal Pension Plans, the funds in personal pension accounts can be invested in financial products such as bank wealth management products, savings deposits, commercial endowment insurance, and public offering funds.
The launch of the first batch of individual pension product funds marks the beginning of individual pension investors being able to officially purchase fund products through their individual pension account.
In November last year, the CBIRC issued the "Provisional Measures for the Management of Personal Pension Business by Commercial Banks and Wealth Management Companies."
The provisions stipulate that participants can purchase various personal pension products, such as personal pension savings, personal pension wealth management products, personal pension insurance products, and public offering fund products for personal pensions, through their personal pension account.
Subsequently, the Ministry of Human Resources and Social Security, the Ministry of Finance, and the State Taxation Administration issued a notice, clearly stating that the individual pension system will be implemented in 36 pilot cities and regions, including Beijing, Tianjin, Shijiazhuang and Xiong'an New Area in Hebei Province, Jincheng City in Shanxi Province, and others.
As of the end of 2022, the number of individuals participating in the individual pension scheme was 19.54 million, with 6.13 million people contributing. The total amount of contributions amounted to 14.2 billion yuan.
Previously, banks participating in the pilot program only listed three types of products: individual pension savings, individual pension insurance products, and public offering funds for individual pensions.
Now that individual pension products have become more diverse, how should investors choose them?
Experts from the Product Development Department of Jiaoyin Wealth Management have stated that, compared to other personal pension products, both the pilot-phase elderly care financial products and the personal pension financial products in the pilot phase have three main characteristics.
First, it is about stability. The risk management mechanism is more sound, the investment strategy and concepts are more prudent, and a third-party independent custody by non-parent institutions is implemented.
Secondly, its long-term nature. The term of pension wealth management products in the pilot phase is all 5 years or more. The next batch of individual pension wealth management products to be issued will have a minimum holding period of 1 year, with encouragement to hold for 3 years or more.
Thirdly, the inclusive nature. The purchase threshold for products is low, with the starting amount usually set at 1 yuan, and some as low as 0.01 yuan. The custody fees, management fees, and other related rates for the products are more favorable.
Experts point out that investors should choose products based on their characteristics and their own risk tolerance.

Recently, the issue of consumer rights protection for members of the well-known fitness chain Zhongjian Fitness has attracted social attention.
After the Spring Festival, many members found that the stores they were located in were not operating normally. Subsequently, rumors about the institution's collapse and disappearance caused panic among the members, leading to the involvement of public security and human resources and social security departments.
Finally, a relevant official from the institution stepped forward to respond, promising to strive to safeguard the legitimate rights and interests of employees and members, and the uproar was eventually calmed down.
Some branches of fitness institutions are experiencing operational problems and have temporarily closed. Consumers could calmly wait for the problem to be resolved. Why do many members' first reaction is "something bad is going to happen"?
The behind-the-scenes controversy involving fitness club members' rights protection reveals the deep-seated issues of trust crises in the industry.
Traditional fitness gyms at home generally adopt a prepayment model, and "earning money that users cannot get back" is the fundamental logic of their development.
By pre-selling membership cards to generate cash flow and not opening new stores, it may seem like a cyclical process, but in reality, it's like eating the rice of the next month to eat today's meal, and cannot form a stable profit model.
Frequent negative news about "running away from the gym" and other industries has greatly shaken consumer confidence.
From the perspective of market prospects, fitness gyms certainly count as a profitable and promising business.
The fitness industry, although experiencing ups and downs, has always attracted the attention of capital.
Multiple data show that the enthusiasm for cultural and sports consumption is continuously rising, and the market is steadily recovering.
Specifically in the fitness market, with the steady improvement of residents' living standards and the deep-rooted concept of a healthy lifestyle, the development of the fitness industry has great potential.
The better the situation, the more the industry needs to pay attention to the quality of development.
However, some institutions are overly optimistic about the fitness market after the recovery of consumption. Having just emerged from the crisis, they once again raise the banner of expansion and invest all their financing funds into the construction of new stores.
It is undeniable that in recent years, due to the impact of the pandemic, many institutions have exited the market, and the industry has undergone a re-shuffling. It is not surprising that some capable enterprises have chosen to increase their acquisition efforts and reposition themselves at this time, which could be a new opportunity.
However, to enter a virtuous cycle and achieve a stable and sustainable development path in the fitness market, the industry must change its traditional model and adjust its business strategies.
Only by firmly taking steady steps and rebuilding consumer trust and sense of security can we truly win the market and the future.
Compared to building new branches to capture the market, the fitness industry currently more needs to focus on optimizing services and using high-quality development to stabilize users.
First and foremost, we need to rely on service to retain users.
GYM is part of the service industry, and its essence is to adhere to the "user-oriented" principle. It should closely revolve around user needs and place the focus of operations on improving and upgrading service quality.
Secondly, we need to rely on digital transformation to enhance the user experience.
For example, the current popular internet gym model allows users to pay per session and choose their own facilities.
At the same time, body data and sports data can be effectively utilized to meet social needs and achieve a complete service loop from online to offline.
There is no personal trainer sales pitch, and no huge annual membership fees. As a result, users' concerns about fitness studios "disappearing" have decreased, and trust has increased.
Additionally, we need to actively explore diverse business models.
Today, sports consumption has moved from single-service consumption to scenario-based consumption. Fitness clubs are expected to become new entry points for consumption. Around the surrounding products related to fitness, such as sports supplies and fitness equipment, may become new profit points. This will provide consumers with more diverse choices.

Nanjing Real Estate Market Reintroduces "Price Difference Compensation" - China News Society
Yanzhijournal News Agency reported (by reporter Ma ZuoBo). Yangzijiang Evening News / Ziniu News reporters found during interviews on the 10th that the "price guarantee agreement," which had not been seen for more than ten years, has returned to the Nanjing real estate market. A residential project in the Jiangbei New Area announced "price difference compensation." Before the delivery of the property, other units' discounts were greater than the previous discounts. The developer is willing to exchange the house for the buyer and refund the price difference.
"Guaranteed Price Agreement" conditions are definitely not to be overlooked.
The residential project located in the Jiangbei New Area is called Pu Luo Yangzi Cuiyuntai. Mr. Sun, a buyer, told reporters that he found a "declaration" while visiting the property on the weekend.
The pictures sent to the journalists show that the developer of the project has promised that if a buyer purchases a unit between February 1 and February 28, the developer can offer the buyer a house exchange and refund the price difference from the date of signing the purchase and sale contract until the delivery of the corresponding unit. If the discount offered for other units in the project is greater than the discount enjoyed by the buyer when purchasing the original unit (limited to other unsold units of the same floor, same building, same unit, and same property type), the developer can exchange the house and refund the price difference.
Mr. Sun said that the sales consultant explained that this is essentially a "price protection agreement," aimed at reassuring buyers that they can confidently proceed with the purchase without worrying about the developer lowering prices for promotion. "The main concern now is that after buying it, the price might drop. If you go to the sales office to complain, there's no time. If you don't complain, it's really heart-wrenching."
Many homebuyers also believe that the "price protection agreement" may seem like a "peace of mind" for homebuyers, but the conditions are not very favorable.
Ms. Zheng, who is planning to buy a necessary housing unit in the Jiangbei area, said that the properties with price guarantees first need to be of the same floor plan, and also need to be in the same building and the same unit. There are very few properties that buyers can compare prices for, so the "barriers" are still quite high.
Some buyers also believe that due to information asymmetry, sellers may not exclude offering additional discounts during sales. It is not easy for buyers to protect their rights and interests.
It remains to be seen whether we can retain homebuyers.
The journalist learned from the Nanjing Online Real Estate website that the developer had obtained a total of 252 units of housing permits for two towers of the project in November last year. The floor areas of the units included 105 square meters, 114 square meters, and 129 square meters, with an average price of 39,843 yuan per square meter for fully furnished units.
However, more than two months have passed, and according to the online real estate data, the total number of unsold units for this project is currently 157, accounting for 62.3%. Among them, Building 8 has a total of 128 units, with 113 still unsold, and Building 7 has 124 units, of which 44 are unsold. The total number of units sold in the project is only 33, accounting for just 13%.
Market participants introduced that "price protection agreements" had also appeared in Nanking in 2008.
In August of that year, the Huizhou Huangshan Road residential project Hengtang Xiyuan aimed to attract buyers' attention and launched a "Price Protection Plan." The specific content was: "The company solemnly promises that if the project's discount increases within one year, the company will refund the difference to existing homeowners."
After April 2011, nine residential projects in Nanjing also offered part of their housing units and promised "price difference compensation".
"The real estate market is showing signs of recovery. At this time, a 'strong heart injection' is more needed."
"A senior operator in the Nanjing real estate industry, Mr. Shen, said, 'Price protection agreements' are also one of the marketing strategies of real estate developers."
He thinks that developers nowadays actually need more confidence than homebuyers. Introducing the "price protection agreement" is also a way to boost their confidence.
As for whether they can retain homebuyers, he said it depends on the influence of multiple factors such as regulatory policies and loan thresholds. At present, it remains to be seen.
Multiple data show a decline; Taiwan's economy performs poorly in the new year - China News Agency
Xinhua News, Taipei, February 11 (Reporter Zhao Bo and Huang Yang) — The export trade volume has been "five consecutive black" (a term referring to a series of negative or declining figures), the manufacturing purchasing managers' index (PMI) has remained in contraction for seven consecutive months, and the consumer price index (CPI) has again hit the "three-digit" level... Recently, the Taiwan authorities have successively released the economic data for January 2023.
Experts' analysis suggests that Taiwan's economy has performed poorly at the beginning of the year, with significant growth pressure.
According to data released by the Taiwan authorities' financial affairs department, Taiwan's exports reached $31.51 billion in January, a year-on-year decline of 21.2%, marking the fifth consecutive month of negative growth.
The export volumes of the 11 major categories of exported goods have all declined compared to the same period last year.
The decline in synthetic rubber and its products, as well as optical instruments, was the most significant, with annual decreases of 37.6% and 35.7%, respectively.
The export of electronic components fell by 20.1% year-on-year, the largest decline in 11 years.
Among them, the integrated circuit industry declined by 18.3%, marking the largest drop in 14 years.
The head of the Statistics Department, Cai Meina, said that this indicates the effect of weak demand in the consumer electronics industry is spreading, and the electronics industry has entered a correction period.
Statistics show that Taiwan's exports and imports both decreased in January, with the surplus amounting to only $2.34 billion, the lowest level in nearly three years.
The Taiwan authorities' financial and economic affairs department believes that global end-market demand has decreased, industrial inventory adjustments have suppressed manufacturers' willingness to purchase raw materials, and the export slump is unlikely to show improvement in the short term. Therefore, Taiwan's first-quarter exports are likely to decline more than expected this year.
According to the Taiwan Economic Research Institute, the seasonally adjusted manufacturing purchasing managers' index (PMI) in Taiwan in January was 40.4%, a decline of 3.3 percentage points from December last year, marking seven consecutive months of contraction.
The PMI (Purchasing Managers' Index) serves as a key indicator in the economic monitoring system, with 50% acting as the critical threshold. An index above 50% indicates expansion of economic activity, while one below 50% signifies contraction.
Among the five components of the Taiwan PMI in January, new orders, production, and labor hiring contracted, while supplier delivery times decreased and inventory tightened.
PMI for all six industries showed signs of contraction. The order of industries by the rate of contraction is as follows: Power and Machinery Industry (35.1%), Electronics and Optics Industry (35.2%), Chemicals and Biotech and Medical Industry (39.2%), Basic Raw Materials Industry (42.4%), Transportation Equipment Industry (42.6%), and Food and Textile Industry (45.7%).
Regarding the price level, the "Central Statistical Office" of the Taiwan authorities announced that the year-on-year inflation rate (CPI) in Taiwan in January was 3.04%, breaking through 3% for the second time since July last year.
Among them, the price of vegetables soared by 28.25%, reaching a new high in 17 months.
Chao Zhihong, a special commissioner of the Taiwan's Bureau of Budget and Accounting, admitted that the rise in vegetable prices has indeed exceeded expectations. Excluding the impact of the Spring Festival, the average CPI for January and February this year should have been close to 2.71% in December last year. Inflation pressure still exists and is still at a high level.
Looking at Taiwan's economy this year, experts express pessimism, according to reports from local media such as "United Daily News" and "Yahoo Finance".
Professor Wu Daren of the Economics Department at Taiwan's National Central University analyzed that export decline may lead to cash flow problems for export manufacturing companies and an increased risk of debt default.
At the same time, the reduction in orders has led to manufacturers cutting labor costs, affecting the labor market and household economies, which in turn impacts consumption capacity.
Chou Daxing, a researcher at the Taiwan Economic Research Institute, believes that with the inflation situation in the United States and Europe still unresolved and demand continuously shrinking, it is expected that Taiwan's exports will continue to decline into the first half of this year.
Since Taiwan's economy relies on exports, the full-year growth performance will be weaker than last year due to sluggish exports.
